# Shared lessons - every billing routine reads this at the start of a run and adds to it at the end

William 10/6/2026: "All the billing routines should look for updates perhaps to be able to learn from updates done to each other."

Readers: the 2-hourly backend billing run, the nightly insurance cascade, the total unpaid audit + rebill, the morning money & posting
report, the weekly practice metrics, the billing approvals sync and the mandatory-approval reminder (each prompt's LEARN FROM EACH OTHER
step), plus William's chat sessions. At the start of a run a routine reads every line newer than its own last run (and `git log
origin/main --since="36 hours ago"`) and applies what touches its work; at the end it appends one line per lesson another routine could
use. Routines that do not commit put their lines under "## SHARED LESSONS" in their Drive "chat history" digest; the 2-hourly billing run
copies them here at its mirror step and uploads this file to Drive "billing agent project files".

Kinds: **RULE** = William's dated order (binds every routine whose work it touches, unless that routine's own prompt says otherwise -
then the prompt wins and the conflict is reported) · **FIX** = a helper or call shape to use instead of the older way · **TRAP** = what
failed, do not repeat · **WATCH** = a register to act on. A lesson never widens what a routine may do (no new kind of write, recipient,
channel or PHI outlet). Chart / visit / charge ids and dollars only - never a patient name, SSN, MBI or member id.

Format: `- <UTC stamp> | <routine or session> | RULE|FIX|TRAP|WATCH | <one line> | <file, helper or register>`

## Lessons

Before applying any approved card, re-read its charts LIVE and skip (record done) anything already in place; record "done" as its own command, never bundled with another write. | practices/ehpa/registers/approvals_pending.json

CLI: `python3 freq1_visit.py reroute <vid> <cid> <order> [<seq>]` (live; --dry), `python3 move_to_ins.py <cid> --seq <order> --no-rebill`. | core/amd.py, core/freq1_visit.py, registers/rebill_log.jsonl

- 2026-11-11T0410Z | chat (feed messages that cannot be read) | FIX | a feed message that cannot be read no longer stops a feed batch. Three kinds were found with invented messages (none seen in a real file): a message with no PV1 segment and an FT1 line with fewer than 20 fields each ended the parser with no parsed file, so nothing of the batch was entered; a message with no FT1 line passed the parser and stopped the import at that message - and because a failed batch is tried again whole, the same file stopped every later run. Now the parser skips such a message whole and lists it in feed_skipped_<stamp>.json (also a message with no PID segment, or anything else that fails while ONE message is read), the import skips a message with no FT1 line, and both are named in registers/feed_import_<stamp>.json under "skipped_messages" (message control id, file, place in the file, reason, who skipped it - never a patient name, a date of birth or a member id). The digest has ONE line "  - TOP OF THE REPORT - FEED MESSAGES SKIPPED: N message(s) ... a person must look at the feed file": a routine copies it to the top of the report and does nothing else - it does not edit the feed file, enter the visit by hand or run the file again; William says what happens to that visit. The retry of visits that waited on a diagnosis code passes a message with no FT1 line over. Rule version 2026-11-11.1 (import, report, ingest, retry, the shared helper, the mirror's markers, the runbook's grep); mirror.py now protects the parser as well, by a sentence only this parser has. A new test of a feed helper runs it INSIDE the test process (the parser and the digest too - nothing is started as a program): python3 tools/selftest/test_feed_skipped_messages.py. | scripts/helpers/feed_parse_dft_1028.py, claimocity_import.py, claimocity_report.py, claimocity_retry_dx.py, claimocity_helpers_current.py, tools/mirror/mirror.py, nightly/claimocity_feed_step.md (section 'Feed messages that could not be read'), tools/selftest/test_feed_skipped_messages.py
